- **Probing found a live regression, not just a roadmap.** `https://www.nseindia.com/api/quote-equity` returns **403 for 6 of 6 symbols** tested. That is the sole supplier of `Industry` and `Mar Cap` to `build_dataset`, which **skips any symbol where either is None** — so **Other Screeners → Pipeline → Sector Rotation is returning an empty frame right now**. *Exactly the failure shape this log recorded for DS-2 three entries down: not an error, just an empty result that looks like an answer.* Filed as **SR-0**, jumping the queue as a bug.
- **What is verified working (2026-08-01, live):** XBRL filing master returns **22 filings for RELIANCE, 28 for TATASTEEL**, quarterly back past Dec 2023 — deeper than the trail's current window; `fetch_ownership` reconciles (`totals_to_100`, promoter+public match master); `fetch_ownership(quarters=N)` already exposes the history API. **Nifty 500 constituent CSV is open** (HTTP 200, 500 rows, carries an `Industry` column). **yfinance `fast_info["market_cap"]`** works (RELIANCE ₹17.7 lakh cr).
- **Two traps recorded so nobody re-walks them:** the filing-master row has its own `industry` key that looks like it routes around the 403 — it is **`'-'` for all 7 symbols tested**. And the taxonomies are **disjoint**: NSE's 20 macro-sectors overlap the 97-key screener.in `SECTOR_INDEX_MAP` at **0 of 20**. A new 20→11 map is needed — cheaper than the 97 it replaces, and coarse labels map onto 11 indices more honestly than a 130-way vocabulary did.
- **Trendlyne, since it was named:** shareholding **does** render without login (verified — 12 quarters of Promoter/FII/DII plus sector/industry labels). Still the wrong primary: **1-decimal values on a quarter-over-quarter *delta*** put the rounding error at the same order as the signal; it is an aggregator where we hold the filing itself; and a requested RELIANCE URL **redirected to a different company**. Their Terms were not read — flagged before any systematic fetch. Demoted to optional backfill (**SR-7**).
- **Recommended order: SR-0 → SR-6 → SR-1 → SR-2 → SR-3 → SR-4 → SR-5.** **SR-6** is the quick win and the honest yes to the yfinance question: a **price-only RRG** (RS-ratio vs RS-momentum with a tail) off the 11 indices `cached_fetch_sector_data` already holds — zero gating, ships standalone. It is **not** a substitute for the ownership axis; [[concepts/FPV-Flow-Price-Volume-Ownership]] is explicit that reading price momentum for ownership flow is the mistake.
- **Nothing built** — all probes read-only.

## [2026-08-01] build | DS-2 SHIPPED — the full ownership split, and the FII fetch never worked
- **The roadmap's only real data gap is closed.** [nse_shareholding_fetch.py](tools/nse_shareholding_fetch.py) now parses the whole SEBI shareholding-pattern XBRL — promoter · FPI Cat I/II · FDI · DII and its sub-legs (mutual funds, insurance, banks, pension, AIF) · retail (≤ and > ₹2 lakh) · NRI · bodies corporate — quarter over quarter, with a **Filter C card** on the Stock Analyzer. DS-3 and DS-4 are now pure composition.
- ### The plan said "mirror the existing FII parser". There was nothing to mirror.
  `parse_fii_pct` tried four regexes that assumed the category name sat **inside the fact's own tag**, and the module's own docstring admitted none of it had ever run against live NSE. Run against a real filing, **all four match nothing**. And because a `None` merely *skips* the symbol, `build_dataset` had been returning an **empty frame for every symbol it was ever pointed at, silently** — the Other Screeners page depends on it. Fixed here as a side effect. *The same failure shape this repo keeps producing: not an error, just an empty result that looks like an answer.*
- **The real structure — the actual discovery.** The XBRL is **dimensional**: one repeated fact tag (`ShareholdingAsAPercentageOfTotalNumberOfShares`) whose category lives in the `contextRef` it points at, as an `explicitMember` on `CategoryOfShareholdersAxis`. Build contextRef→member first, read facts through it, and the **entire** split falls out instead of one number. Only **single-member** contexts are read — filings stack a shareholder-name dimension for individual holders, which would otherwise double-count into their own category.
- **The BSE scripcode blocker was sidestepped, not solved.** [[sources/BSEIndia-Public-API-Corporate-Data-2026]] listed NSE-symbol→BSE-scripcode mapping as a prerequisite for BSE's shareholding endpoint. **NSE serves the same `in-bse-shp` XBRL keyed by trading symbol**, so nothing needed mapping — and the scrip code turns out to be *inside* the file as the entity identifier (RELIANCE = 500325).
- **Cross-checks travel with the data.** The filing-master row independently reports promoter and public %, so every parse is reconciled against them and carries a `reconciles` flag; the card warns loudly when it is false. Verified across **RELIANCE / TCS / TITAN / CUPID / SHADOWFAX** (large, mid, small, recent listing) — all reconcile to the pp, all total 100.00%.
- **Two more bugs found while reading the old code:** filings were sorted on a `toDate` key **these records don't have** (ordering was luck), and a **revised** filing is republished under the same period date and would have read as an extra quarter with zero change. Now sorted on the parsed period and de-duplicated.
- **Live rotations differ meaningfully:** CUPID **SMART MONEY IN** (FII +3.16pp, retail −0.72pp — and it sits at **#2 on the stored Opportunity Hub shortlist**); RELIANCE **DISTRIBUTION** (FII −1.48pp while retail +0.22pp); TITAN **MIXED**.
- **Deliberately stops short of a signal.** `interpret_rotation` reads ownership only. The framework's actual gate — "FII accumulating into a **weak** market = watchlist, not a buy" — needs market health (**DS-3**), and the FPV combinations need price+volume (**DS-4**). The card says so, because reading a rotation alone is precisely the mistake [[concepts/FPV-Flow-Price-Volume-Ownership]] warns about. **Mutual funds are a subset of DII** — the card states that the two legs overlap and don't add.
- **Quarterly and lagging by design**, so the filing period leads the card rather than hiding in a footnote (the wiki's "price levels decay" rule). Cached 24h — the underlying data changes once a quarter. **CLI:** `py -3 tools/nse_shareholding_fetch.py --ownership RELIANCE TITAN CUPID`.

## [2026-08-01] build | DS-1 SHIPPED — the market-health gate now names all five conditions
- New [market_health.py](tools/market_health.py) — **Filter A** of [[strategies/Crash-Proof-3-Filter-System]] as its own engine, with the framework's own rule: **2 or more of 5 fail → STAND DOWN**. Three of the five (breakouts working, leaders strong, volatility) were **unmodelled anywhere in the repo** before this.
- **Three states, never two.** Every condition returns PASS / FAIL / **NOT_CHECKED**, and NOT_CHECKED never counts toward the gate — a condition we could not measure must not read as one that held (the [red_flags.py](tools/red_flags.py) discipline, reused). The verdict always reports how many of the five were *actually checked*, and **0 checked returns "NOT ASSESSED", not "CLEAR"** — returning a green light nobody computed is the exact failure `market_verdict` warns about.
- **How each condition is measured:** ① NIFTY vs its **50 EMA** (the framework's level — note the ribbon uses the 200 SMA); ② breadth read off the **already-persisted** sentiment reading rather than a second bhavcopy pull; ③ share of names that closed above their prior 20-day high in the last 10 sessions and are **still above that breakout close**; ④ share of the **top-quintile by trailing RS** — the same primitive the scanners and the Opportunity Hub rank with — still above their 21 EMA; ⑤ **India VIX** vs its 90-day median with an **absolute floor of 15**, falling back to realized vol and *saying so* when the VIX fetch fails.
- **The absolute floor is the reused lesson.** A ratio-only test flips on any uptick from a calm baseline — `classify_regime` documents that exact trap for realized vol (NIFTY at 12% tripped VOLATILE against a 9.6% baseline). Both tests must pass before volatility reads elevated. Verified: VIX 12.00 against a 9.00 median stays **calm** because it is under 15.
- **Two guard rails against a data fault wearing a market result's clothes** — the recurring failure shape in this repo. A basket whose fetch returned under **50% coverage** reports NOT_CHECKED rather than measuring whichever names survived; a tape with fewer than **5 breakouts** reports NOT_CHECKED rather than scoring the silence as "breakouts fail".
- **Deliberately NOT folded into the ribbon verdict.** Breadth and regime feed both engines, so adding these failures to `build_verdict`'s caution count would penalise the same weakness twice with no lamp explaining it. Fusing them is DS-3's job, where the interaction is the point. It renders as a **second opinion beside** the composite.
- **The two engines disagreed on day one, and correctly.** Live 2026-08-01: the ribbon reads **STAND DOWN** while Filter A reads **CLEAR (1 of 5 failed)** — NIFTY sits **+1.52% above its 50 EMA** but **−2.17% below its 200 SMA**, so the framework's level passes where the ribbon's fails. That divergence is the feature: it is arguable per condition instead of in aggregate.
- **Verified live.** Unit-tested all five conditions on synthetic frames across pass/fail/not-checked including both guard rails; gate arithmetic checked at 0/1/2/4 failures and at zero-checked. Live read with a 60-name basket (56/60 coverage): 50 EMA **PASS** (+1.52%), breadth **FAIL** (700 adv vs 1,682 decl, 29%), breakouts **PASS** (32/50 holding, 64%), leaders **PASS** (11/11 above their 21 EMA), volatility **PASS** (VIX 11.76 vs 16.41 median). Five lamps render on the Market Sentiment page in the right colours; zero console errors.
- **Cost split by design:** conditions 1/2/5 are ~free (two index fetches + a DB row) and render on page load; 3/4 need a 60-name basket and sit behind a **"Run basket checks"** button, cached 30 min. **CLI:** `py -3 tools/market_health.py --basket 60`.
- **None of these thresholds are backtested** — the strategy page's caveat still stands, and DS-2 (the full FII/DII/MF/Retail split) remains the next item and the real data gap.

## [2026-07-31] build | Opportunity Hub automated into a stored daily top-10 (`b4f24d7`)
- **Trigger: the user said they didn't understand the Hub.** Fair — it is a **five-step** funnel (pick universe → build → choose sectors → choose industries → pick **one of 24** scanners → scan) that **persists nothing**, so every session starts from an empty screen and yesterday's shortlist is gone. New [opportunity_daily.py](tools/opportunity_daily.py) runs the *same* funnel unattended and stores the result, turning the daily question into "what did the funnel like today" instead of "which of 24 scanners should I try".
- **"Really an opportunity" = three things agreeing**, and the score says so: **location** (in a leading sector *and* industry — a hard filter, not a bonus) · **confluence** (>1 scanner fired) · **quality** (M1 Overall Rating). `score = 0.45×rating + 0.30×best-scanner-score + 0.25×confluence`.
- **Scanners are NOT weighted equally — the backtest finally pays off.** [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] measured most of them and only a few beat a random-entry baseline: **Falling Wedge** and **Harmonic Reversal** did, **Order Block** showed +0.11R, while RSI-60-40 / VCP / Structural Breakout came in **at or below zero**. Those three count **double**; treating all 24 alike would let the ones *measured as worthless* outvote the ones that worked. When every hit on a name is an unmeasured scanner, the stored note says so in plain words ("treat as a lead, not a signal").
- **New `daily_opportunities` table** in [portfolio_db.py](tools/portfolio_db.py) — one row per symbol per run date, so it **doubles as a track record**. `market_regime` + `market_verdict` are stored **with each row**: a top-10 produced while the index sits under its 200 SMA is not the same artifact as one from a healthy tape, and in six months nobody will remember which was which. A re-run **replaces** that date rather than appending.
- **Refactor: the M1 rating moved to [rating.py](tools/rating.py).** It lived inside `dashboard_app.py` and could therefore only run inside a Streamlit session; the headless job needs the **same** rating the interactive Hub shows, and a second implementation would guarantee eventual disagreement (the same one-scorer rule as `fundamental_score.py`). `dashboard_app` imports it under the old private names — **all 14 call sites untouched**.
- ### Robustness bug the first live run exposed
  `build_constituent_table` fans `.info` calls across **8 threads**, and Yahoo answers a burst with **HTTP 401 "Invalid Crumb"**. Because every failure is swallowed per-symbol, the result is **not an error** — it's an empty or half-empty table that would quietly rank sectors from whatever survived. The first run tagged **0 of 60** and reported only "no stocks could be tagged". The job now uses **4 workers**, measures the **tag rate**, and if it's under **35%** pauses 45s and retries with 2 — after which a 40-name run tagged **78%**. *Same failure shape as the NaN forming bar: a data fault that looks like a market result.*
- **Dashboard:** the stored shortlist now **leads** the Opportunity Hub page — run-date selector over history, "Run daily shortlist now", the market-regime banner read from the stored row, and per-name cards (rank · rating badge · score · confluence · industry/sector · plain-language reason). The manual funnel is unchanged below it. **CLI:** `py -3 tools/opportunity_daily.py --universe 200 --top 10`.
- **Verified live:** a 120-name run stored **10 rows for 2026-07-31**, labelled **RISK-OFF** (Nifty −1.6% vs its 200 SMA), with **pharma + consumer-defensive** leadership — coherent for that tape. TORNTPHARM #1 on 5 scanners / 86-100 rating; LTFOODS made it on a **single** Order Block hit and is scored accordingly at #9. Page renders banner, cards and badges; no server exceptions.
- **Still open:** nothing schedules this yet — the existing 4:05 PM Task Scheduler job runs `screener_pipeline`, not this. Registering a second task is a machine-config change and was left for the user to approve.

## [2026-07-31] build | FR-5 SHIPPED — forward scenarios, with the arithmetic printed under every bar
- New [scenarios.py](tools/scenarios.py) (`e027dd0`). **Arithmetic, not a forecast** — and built so the page can *prove* it: every bar carries its own multiplier as a printable string (`₹78,213 Cr × (1 + 3.6%)^3 = ₹87,094 Cr; × 26.4% margin = ₹23,036 Cr`), so a reader can redo it by hand instead of trusting it.
- **Bands come from the company's own filed history, not an invented ±X%.** Base = observed 3Y revenue CAGR at the latest margin · Bull = best single filed year at the best filed margin · Bear = worst at worst. The spread is therefore a statement about *this company's* volatility. Four annual columns give **three growth observations**, so the band is wide and noisy — the caption says so rather than hiding it behind a smooth chart.
- **Net profit, not NOPAT** — a documented deviation. NOPAT needs EBIT and an effective tax rate separated; this stack has neither cleanly (tax provision isn't exposed, and `net_income ÷ EBIT` conflates interest), so the projection uses **net margin** (post-tax by definition) and labels the output honestly.
- ### Three flaws that only appeared when run on real names
  1. **ITC's "bull" margin was 46.5% — the FY25 hotels-demerger year**, against a 30.4% median. Projecting a one-off gain as a *sustainable* margin made the bull profit bar more than double the base. Arithmetic left alone; the **outlier is disclosed**, naming the year it came from.
  2. **SWIGGY's Bull bar landed BELOW its Base bar.** The growth cap hit only the bull (best year 51.7% → 40%) while the base rode its 41% CAGR uncapped. If sustaining 40%+ for three years is implausible enough to cap, **it is implausible in the base case too** — the cap now applies to every rate, and **bull ≥ base ≥ bear is enforced by construction**.
  3. **Compounding an exceptional year turns a bar chart into a cartoon**, so growth is capped at **40%/yr** with the uncapped figure printed — the same plausibility guard [projection.py](tools/projection.py) already applies to price (the M3 lesson, reused).
- **"1 Thing to Track" is rule-based, not editorial.** Each input is varied across its own bull-to-bear range with the other held at base; whichever produces the larger profit spread is named, with a **numeric threshold**. ITC → margin-driven (**₹17,497 Cr** of swing vs ₹9,856 Cr for growth); TITAN → growth-driven.
- **Kept visibly distinct from the chart's price projection.** `projection.py` projects **price** from Elliott-Wave / measured-move geometry; this projects the **size of the business** from filed revenue and margin. Different objects, different units, and the caption states that **neither confirms the other** — a reader who conflated them would think two independent methods agreed.
- **Verified live.** ITC bars measured in the DOM at **100% / 81.9% / 65%** width, exactly proportional to projected revenue, in green/blue/red; the demerger-margin guard renders; the "1 thing to track" card carries the **2px amber emphasis border, used once** on the page. SWIGGY renders the capped + negative-profit case coherently (bull *and* base both disclose the cap; bear bar 91.4%). Empty / single-year / no-YoY inputs return `None` → explicit "not enough filed history" panel rather than extrapolating from one observation. Zero console errors, no server exceptions.
- **FR-6 (narrative / concall) is the only phase left**, and it is the one with no LLM configured — deliberately last since the M3 finding. **Restart start.bat** to pick this up.

## [2026-07-30] build | FR-4 SHIPPED — red flags, and the third state is the whole point
- New [red_flags.py](tools/red_flags.py) (`8ae52f2`). **A checklist showing only CLEAN and FLAG is dishonest** — auditor qualifications, related-party transactions and revenue concentration live in annual-report *prose* that nothing in this stack parses, so omitting them reads as "we looked and it was clean". Every check now returns **FLAG / CLEAN / NOT_CHECKED**, and NOT_CHECKED is never a pass.
- **Eight checks, three tiers of sourcing:** *free* (negative-FCF streak, rising debtor days, rising working-capital days — off the FR-1 statement layer) · *session-gated* (promoter stake reduction, promoter pledging — screener.in cookie; **NOT_CHECKED without one, rather than vanishing**, so the unchecked governance surface stays visible) · *permanently unsourceable* (auditor / RPT / revenue concentration, with the reason printed).
- **`no_critical_flags()` refuses to return True unless every critical check actually ran.** With three permanently unsourceable critical items it returns **False for every symbol today**, so the source mockup's green "✅ No Critical Red Flags" panel is replaced by an honest **"3 of 8 checks could run"** panel. That is the correct answer, not a bug — this stack cannot clear a company of auditor risk.
- **Two calibration decisions.** Trend flags need **≥3 yearly observations** (yfinance returns 4–5 annual columns; two points is not a trend). And a trend only flags once the **absolute level** matters — debtor days going 4→7 is +75% and meaningless — so floors sit at **45d** (debtor) and **120d** (working capital), beneath which a large % move stays CLEAN *with the arithmetic shown*.
- **Data layer:** per-year debtor-day and WC-day series, with balance-sheet and income columns **matched by date** — the two frames don't always carry the same periods, and pairing one year's receivables with another year's revenue would invent a trend.
- **Rows render FLAG → NOT CHECKED → CLEAN**, deliberately not the flattering order: an unexamined risk deserves more attention than a check that passed.
- **Verified live.** MAPMYINDIA: debtor days **+80% over 4 years (76d → 136d)** flagged, WC −18% clean → 1 flagged / 2 clean / 5 not checked. SWIGGY: **4 consecutive years of negative FCF** flagged *critical* (red) + debtor days +36% flagged *watch* (amber) → 2 / 1 / 5. ITC clean on everything checkable. Severity visually distinct in the DOM (`#dc2626` critical · `#d97706` watch · `#9ca3af` not-checked). Zero console errors, no server exceptions.
- **Honest gap: the screener.in branch never ran end-to-end** — this machine has **no screener.in session cookie**, so the live company-page fetch was never exercised. Its *decision logic* was unit-tested against a **synthetic company page through the real parser** (`screener_scraper._parse_quarterly_holding`): a 54.20% → 50.90% promoter slide flags critical, 7.5% pledged flags critical, a stable stake + 0% pledge return CLEAN, and an **absent pledge row returns NOT_CHECKED rather than 0%**. The network path remains unverified until a session exists.
- **FR-5 and FR-6 remain open.** **Restart start.bat** to pick it up.

## [2026-07-30] ingest | Article (pasted) — "How to Capture Strong Trends with Moving Averages"
- **Low-yield ingest, and worth saying so.** Ten sections and seven "techniques", of which **six were already on [[concepts/EMA]]** — and the repo's **MA Bias** chart overlay (`maBiasCompute` in [chart_dashboard.html](tools/chart_dashboard.html)) already implements a **stricter four-tier** version of the whole framework (regime → stack quality → trigger → distrust). The `/learn/moving-averages-deep-dive` lesson was ingested on **2026-06-22** via [[sources/SwingEdge-Learn-Indicators-Deep-Dive]].
- **Attribution deliberately left unverified.** Pasted immediately after a request to capture `swingedge.info` and overlapping that lesson heavily — but the text has **no byline** and reads like a generated summary, so it was filed as its own source ([[sources/MA-Trend-Capture-Guide-Pasted]]) rather than folded into the SwingEdge page, to avoid baking in a wrong attribution.
- **The one genuine contribution — the named no-trade zone.** Requiring **both** fast-above-slow **and** price-above-fast, and naming the state where only the first holds (`MA20 > MA200` but price < `MA20`) as an explicit stand-aside. Most write-ups stop at the crossover and never name the in-between state, which is exactly where whipsaws are manufactured. Merged into [[concepts/EMA]] as a 4-row matrix.
- **Two gap-fills:** the **100-period MA** (missing from our period table — sits between the twitchy 50 and the slow 200) and **MA-as-a-zone** (plot a pair, or offset one MA by a small %, and treat the band as S/R — the same instinct as the overlay's ATR "braided" test).
- **One claim rejected — "fast-MA slope > 45° = strong trend" (`atan(slope)×180/π`).** An angle in degrees is **not a property of the data**: it depends on chart aspect ratio, zoom, and linear-vs-log axis. The same series reads 20° or 70° by dragging the window edge, and a ₹50 stock cannot share a threshold with a ₹5,000 one. **We already do this correctly** — `extAtr` / `braidAtr` normalise against ATR. Kept the idea (slope encodes strength; a flattening fast MA warns before price does — already on the page since 2026-06-22), discarded the units.
- **One risk claim resisted** — "all MAs stacked → increase position size" conflicts with [[analyses/Kalyan-Trading-Philosophy]] and [[strategies/Crash-Proof-3-Filter-System]], where size follows **regime and risk-per-trade**, not indicator alignment. A full stack is a reason to *take* the trade, not to enlarge it.
- Pages updated: [[concepts/EMA]] (new section + warning callout; frontmatter `sources:` also corrected — it read 1 while three were listed), [[sources/MA-Trend-Capture-Guide-Pasted]] (new), index.md (198 pages / 81 sources). **No code change, no new scanner** — nothing here justified one.

## [2026-07-30] build | FR-3 SHIPPED — history surfaces + a live-data NaN fix that was corrupting the whole analyzer
- **FR-3 renders what FR-1 already fetched** (`1ed75ab`) — nothing new is downloaded. **Annual table** (revenue · YoY · net profit · YoY · net margin · OPM · EPS) and a **quarterly EPS card grid** (EPS headline, revenue + profit-YoY beneath, labelled by **Indian fiscal quarter** — `2026-03-31` → `Q4 FY26`), with |YoY| ≥ 40% annotated as an outlier or a sharp drop. **Two surfaces on purpose:** years are a *comparison* (table), quarters are read one at a time with their own annotations (cards).
- **"Consensus data unavailable" ships as copy, not as a build** — no free NSE consensus feed exists, so the panel says the page cannot show a beat/miss record instead of approximating one.
- **Honest labelling:** the best quarter is "**highest of the N shown**", never "record quarter" — yfinance returns ~5 quarters and an all-time claim would be unsupported. Gapped quarters read "**no year-ago quarter filed**" rather than inventing a comparison. Annual YoY only fills when the adjacent column is genuinely **300–430 days** older; trailing all-NaN annual columns are dropped (an empty column is not a year of zero earnings).
- ### The bug this surfaced — a NaN forming bar was corrupting the entire analyzer
  During a live session **yfinance returns a bar for TODAY with volume but NaN OHLC**. One NaN close poisoned everything at once: the header rendered **₹nan**, `score_minervini` failed every comparison against it (**0/8 where the truth was 1/8**), `score_technofunda` read **1/6 instead of 4/6**, and CANSLIM printed **+nan% vs Nifty**. Measured on ITC: last bar `2026-07-30` with `volume 18,282,297` and NaN open/high/low/close.
  - **This is the fourth time this repo has hit the same class of bug** — `scan_pead`, `volume_profile` and the Opportunity Hub momentum ranking each grew their own `dropna` downstream. The fix therefore went in **`cached_fetch_ohlcv` + `cached_fetch_nifty50`** (new `_drop_unusable_bars`), so **every page inherits it** rather than the next consumer rediscovering it.
  - **Worth remembering:** the failure is silent and looks like a *market* signal, not a data fault — a stock "failing every Minervini condition" reads as a weak stock, not as a broken frame. Any new consumer of a price frame should assume a forming bar exists.
- **Verified live on ITC after the fix:** ₹286.25 · +0.56% · price as of **2026-07-29** (the last completed session) · Minervini **1/8** · TechnoFunda **4/6** · RS **−24.3%** vs Nifty · **no NaN anywhere on the page**. Quarter cards read Q4 FY26 EPS 4.30 (+9.3% profit YoY) through Q3 FY25.
- **Verification caveat, stated plainly:** the annual table's cells were checked through the **identical data path**, not on screen — Streamlit renders dataframes to **canvas**, so cells are unreadable from the DOM, and the Browser pane was not compositing for a screenshot. ITC FY25 shows net margin 46.5% / EPS 27.79 (the **hotels-demerger** year) then **−40.5%** profit YoY into FY26; TITAN FY26 EPS **57.19** agrees with the header's TTM **57.23**.
- **FR-4…FR-6 remain open.** **Restart start.bat** to pick it up.

## [2026-07-30] build | FR-2 SHIPPED — the one fundamental scorer + six signal donuts
- New [fundamental_score.py](tools/fundamental_score.py) (`50532a1`) — **the single band scorer both roadmaps insisted on**. The report wants six donuts and [[analyses/Radar-Chart-Analyzer-Fit]] RC-1 wants four axes over the same ideas; scoring them apart would give the dashboard **two fundamental opinions**. Every band is an explicit constant with its reasoning beside it, calibrated for **NSE** listed equity (quality compounders routinely trade at P/E 50–90 — US bands would mark the whole market overvalued).
- **Six axes:** Valuation · Growth · Health · Returns · **Capital Allocation** · **Earnings Consistency**. Capital Allocation only became computable because FR-1 fetched the **cash-flow statement** (FCF margin + ROCE trend).
- **No overall number, on purpose.** The page already carries M1's technical /100 and the council verdict; a fourth "fundamental /100" would read as a competing master score. Output is a per-axis score + verdict word + the **good-business-vs-bad-entry split** as two words. TITAN: **GOOD QUALITY business at an OVERVALUED entry** — exactly the pairing the split exists to keep visible.
- **Three calibration flaws found by running it on real names, and fixed:**
  1. **Loss-makers scored CHEAP.** No earnings → no P/E and no PEG → P/B was the only input, and **SWIGGY came out "CHEAP"** while burning cash with 0/4 profitable years. Valuation now returns an explicit **`NO EARNINGS`** state — unscored, but for a *stated reason* rather than a silent gap.
  2. **Health averaged leverage away.** TITAN's **1.95× D/E** was offset by tight working capital into "STRONG". Health now blends mean with the **worst sub-score 50/50** (`agg="min_drag"`) — a balance-sheet risk is not compensable. TITAN → SOLID 57.
  3. **Working-capital days was the wrong health metric entirely.** yfinance's `Working Capital` = current assets − current liabilities, so it **includes cash and current investments**: MAPMYINDIA, debt-free and cash-rich, scored **STRETCHED for being well funded**. Swapped to **debtor days**, which measures real collection stress and cannot be flattered by a cash pile. ITC → STRONG 100 (D/E 0.03×, 18 debtor days).
- **Also fixed a real bug in FR-1's quarterly YoY.** It compared **4 columns back**, but yfinance's quarterly frames have **gaps** — TITAN's is missing `2025-09-30` — so March was compared against the previous **December**, reading **+17% where the true YoY was +48%**. Now **matched by date** (±45d tolerance); no match → no YoY. The Growth axis prefers this transparent figure over yfinance's opaque `revenueGrowth` (which claims **80.5%** for the same quarter).
- **Verified live.** TITAN: Valuation 10 OVERVALUED · Growth 93 STRONG · Health 57 SOLID · Returns 60 GOOD · Capital Allocation 60 BALANCED · Earnings Consistency 79 CONSISTENT · coverage **16/17** — all seven SVG arcs measured in the DOM fill **exactly** their score with the correct tone colour. SWIGGY renders the absent path (grey arc, no centre value, `NO EARNINGS` + explanation, **13/17**). ITC **17/17**. Empty / P/E-only / synthetic loss-making inputs all degrade without fabricating a verdict. Zero console errors, no server exceptions. *(No screenshot — the Browser pane was not compositing; proof is the DOM read-back.)*
- **Earnings Consistency measures REALISED results** (profitable years · growth years · margin stability), **not** beats against consensus — no free NSE consensus source exists ([[analyses/PEAD-Scanner-Roadmap]]), and the caption says so rather than implying a clean beat record.
- **FR-3…FR-6 remain open.** RC-1 must now consume `score_all()` rather than growing its own bands.
- **Restart start.bat** to pick it up.

## [2026-07-29] build | SBC chart overlay SHIPPED — Structure Break Chain (BOS/CHoCH + quality)
- Built the chart overlay for the just-ingested [[sources/Structure-Break-Chain-SBC-Pine]] (© SharpStrat, **MPL 2.0** — ported with attribution in the docstring). Went straight to the chart per the user's ask, so this is **SBC-4 ahead of SBC-1/2/3** — the scanner/Setup-Intelligence wiring and the backtest remain open.
- **New detector** `detect_structure_chain(df, pivot_len=15, confirm, fail_win, max_swings)` in [scanner_templates.py](tools/scanner_templates.py), beside `detect_bos_choch` (**unchanged** — no consumer touched). Where the old one returns the single latest *bullish* event, this walks the series and returns **every** BOS/CHoCH in both directions with SBC's three gradings: **quality** (`0.6·dist/ATR14 + 0.4·body/ATR14` → STRONG ≥0.75 / NORMAL ≥0.35 / WEAK), **chain** (consecutive same-direction breaks since the last CHoCH), **failed** (close back inside within N bars). Faithful to the source: one event per bar with bullish priority, level consumed after the break, failure always close-tested and never on the break bar, completed-chain averages accumulated only at CHoCH from a pre-mutation snapshot. Reuses `_atr_series` (Wilder, = `ta.atr`). One documented deviation: pivots use this module's window-max rule (a flat plateau can register) rather than Pine's strict comparison.
- **New endpoint** `/api/structure_chain` on [chart_server.py](tools/chart_server.py) — maps bar indices → chart timestamps, with a **per-timeframe pivot default** (1d/1w 15 · 1h 10 · 15m 8 · 1m 5, same shape as `/api/trendlines`) and `confirm` / `fail_win` / `max_swings` query params.
- **Client overlay** ([chart_dashboard.html](tools/chart_dashboard.html)): new `structure_chain` indicator (Pattern Recognition) → `renderStructureChain`/`clearStructureChain` draw 12 dashed swing levels, one marker per event (arrowUp/arrowDown for BOS, circle for CHoCH, purple square for FAILED at the bar it broke back), and a collapsible **chain card** in the existing `.action-stack` (order 5) showing trend, live chain, last event + quality + age, level broken, BOS/CHoCH totals, chain max/avg, failed count, and the parameter line. **Quality is encoded as marker opacity** (STRONG 1.0 / NORMAL 0.72 / WEAK 0.4 — the source's 0/28/60 transparency), so weak breaks recede instead of adding a fourth label. Markers capped at the last 30 events for legibility.
- **Verified live in the browser** (chart_server :5001, TITAN.NS): toggling ON → **47 markers read back off the series** (`series.markers().length`, not just our own state) + **12 swing series each with 2 points** + the card reading "Structure BULLISH · chain 5 · BOS WEAK ✗ 13b ago · 23/22 BOS/CHoCH · chain max 7 / avg 1.8 · 24 failed"; opacity mapping confirmed in the marker colours (`rgba(0,230,118,1)` on a STRONG break vs `rgba(0,230,118,0.4)` on a WEAK one, with the purple `✗ FAILED` square 1 bar later). Toggling OFF → **0 markers, state nulled, card removed from the DOM, no leaked series**. Intraday re-render clean (1h: pivot 10, 18 markers, 12 swings). **Zero console errors** throughout; py_compile clean.
- **Parameter behaviour measured** (600 daily bars, TITAN): `confirm=wick` is demonstrably noisier than close — CHoCH **6 → 13**, failed **7 → 15**, max chain **5 → 3** — which is the source's "faster, noisier" claim, now quantified on our data. `fail_win=0` correctly disables failure detection (0 flagged), and a too-short window returns `{"structure_chain": null}` → the client clears.
- **Honest caveats.** (1) No screenshot: the Browser pane wasn't displaying, so visual proof is the DOM/LWC read-back above, not an image. (2) I **restarted chart_server** to load the new route (the running instance had `debug=False`, no reloader) — it is up on :5001 with the new code. (3) The overlay runs over the chart's full loaded history (2600 bars on 1d), which is faithful to Pine but means the totals describe a decade, not a recent window — the card labels "13b ago" so the live read stays honest. (4) **Nothing is scored or filtered by this yet** — quality/chain/failed are displayed only. **SBC-3 (does quality ≠ WEAK or chain ≥ N actually lift `scan_order_block`'s +0.11R or rescue Structural Breakout's ≤0 edge?) is still the load-bearing question** and is unanswered.
- **Restart start.bat / hard-refresh the chart** to pick it up in a normal session.

## [2026-07-29] ingest | Pine v6 — Structure Break Chain [SBC] (SharpStrat)
- Ingested a pasted Pine v6 indicator → [[sources/Structure-Break-Chain-SBC-Pine]]; folded its new mechanics into [[concepts/Market-Structure-CHOCH-BOS]] (sources 2→3, new "Grading the break" section) and pointed [[analyses/Dashboard-UI-Roadmap]] Phase 2 at the port plan. **License MPL 2.0 → a port is fine with attribution** (unlike the CC BY-NC-SA LuxAlgo script).
- **Naming flag first:** despite the request calling it a "structural breakout indicator," this is **not** our `scan_structural_breakout` (a multi-week horizontal-resistance base breakout). SBC is swing-structure BOS/CHoCH labelling with a continuation **chain counter**. Kept the names separate.
- **Three contributions our `detect_bos_choch` lacks** (it returns only `{signal, level, bars_ago}`, bullish-only, close-confirmed, `pivot_len=5`): **(1) break quality** = `0.6×(dist÷ATR14) + 0.4×(|close−open|÷ATR14)` → STRONG ≥0.75 / NORMAL ≥0.35 / WEAK (60% how far beyond the level, 40% how decisive the candle — symbol-agnostic, free); **(2) chain count** of consecutive BOS since the last CHoCH, where the CHoCH reset *is* the character change (completed-chain averages accumulate at CHoCH from a pre-mutation snapshot, so the in-progress chain is excluded — an honest choice); **(3) time-boxed failed break** — close back inside the level within 3 bars → FAILED, which our port has no fast path for (ours waits for a full counter-structure break).
- **Two design questions it exposes in our own port:** SBC uses **15-bar pivots**, LuxAlgo **5 internal + 50 swing**, ours **5** — so we may be labelling *internal* structure while reading it as major structure (a substantive disagreement, not tuning). And SBC treats **wick-vs-close confirmation** as a user choice ("faster, noisier") where we hard-coded close without measuring. Also noted: one structural event per bar with bull priority; failure always close-tested even when breaks are wick-confirmed; `i_fail_win=0` silently disables the check; quality encoded as label **opacity** (a presentation idea worth stealing).
- **Paste was truncated** (ends mid chain-counter block): the dashboard panel, pullback entry zones, HTF overlay, background shading and all alert calls are declared by inputs but **absent from the code** — documented as intent-only, not as mechanics. `prev_sh`/`prev_sl` are tracked but unused in the pasted body, presumably feeding the missing zone logic.
- Plan **SBC-1** (additive keys — `quality`/`chain`/`failed`/`direction` + configurable `confirm` & `pivot_len`; the three existing consumers — SMC checklist Structure check, `scan_order_block`, the `BOS/CHOCH` tag — keep working) → **SBC-2** (gate the Structure check on quality ≠ WEAK, chain → Setup Intelligence persistence factor, failed → fail-risk chip on the cards) → **SBC-3 measure before adopting** ([[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] put Structural Breakout at ≤0 edge and `scan_order_block` at +0.11R; both consume this detector, so the question "does quality/chain actually lift them" is cheap and decisive) → **SBC-4** chart overlay only if SBC-3 pays. Reuses `_atr_series` (same Wilder smoothing as `ta.atr`) — no new dependency, no new data source. **Nothing built.**

## [2026-07-25] fix | Thin BSE/SME symbols render 1 candle — yfinance retry-on-stub
- **Symptom (user):** Diamond Power (DIACABS.BO) and Vilas Transcore "not appearing properly" — chart showed a single candle.
- **Diagnosis (measured):** the data path is actually sound — DIACABS.BO returns **2600 bars** and VILAS is rescued to VILAS.NS (**534 bars**) by the endpoint's existing cross-exchange fallback. The 1-candle was a **transient yfinance stub**: yfinance intermittently returns a 1-3 row stub for thinly-traded BSE/SME names, and if BOTH exchanges stub at the same moment the cross-exchange fallback can't rescue it → 1 candle. The fetch never *retried* the same symbol.
- **Fix** ([data_source.py](tools/data_source.py) `YFinanceSource.get_ohlcv`): retry the download **up to 3× on empty-or-stub** (`empty` or, for a real multi-year request `bars≥20`, `<5` rows), 0.4s/0.8s backoff *outside* the serialization lock. A plain re-fetch almost always succeeds, so transient stubs now self-heal before the chart ever sees them.
- **Verified:** py_compile clean; no regression (DIACABS.BO 2600, VILAS 534, VILAS.NS 534 post-patch); browser end-to-end — DIACABS.BO now renders **full 2600-bar history (2016→2026)**, not 1 candle. **Restart start.bat** to load it.
- **Separate minor note:** Vilas Transcore isn't in the autocomplete symbol directory (`search('vilas')` → no hits), so it won't *suggest* — but typing `VILAS` (or `VILAS.NS`) + Enter loads it via the endpoint fallback. Adding it to the directory would only improve discoverability, not loading.

## [2026-07-25] fix | Chart sub-pane alignment — right edge static, symmetric shrink
- **Symptom (user):** shrinking/zooming the chart moved both edges and the oscillator sub-panes (RSI/MACD/…) drifted "out of window side by side" instead of tracking the main chart. Wanted: right edge pinned, shrink from the left, sub-panes symmetric.
- **Root cause (measured in-browser):** indicator sub-series **skip their warm-up bars** (RSI(14) starts 14 bars in), so each sub-pane sat on a *shorter bar grid* than the main. The sub-pane's last logical index was < the main's → the old **time-range** sub-sync drifted (sub right-edge sat ~14 bars behind, showing a wider window). Confirmed live: main `{2550,2599}` vs RSI `{2480,2585}`.
- **Fix** ([chart_dashboard.html](tools/chart_dashboard.html)): (1) new `padToBars()` pads every sub-pane primary series with leading whitespace to the **full bar grid** so logical index i = same bar in main and every sub; (2) switched all sub-pane sync from time-range to **logical-range** (`setVisibleLogicalRange`), centralised in `syncSubPaneRanges` (resize / pan-zoom / reload / async-RS all route through it); (3) added `rightPriceScale.minimumWidth: 72` to main + sub opts so the price axes (and thus plot areas) are equal-width → bars align pixel-for-pixel.
- **Verified live** (ITC, RSI+MACD): main + both sub-panes share the **identical** logical range (`{2562,2599}`), right & left edges match by timestamp, all axis widths = 72, zero console errors. Before: RSI right-edge 14 bars behind; after: locked 1:1. Minor trade-off: the 72px axis reserves a little more width (fewer bars in the narrow 4-up preview; negligible full-size). **Restart start.bat / hard-refresh the chart** to load it.

## [2026-07-25] build | FW-4 SHIPPED — Falling Wedge backtest (has measured edge)
- Ran the [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]] on "Falling Wedge" (49 liquid names, 1500 bars, ~48s). The scanner's structured entry/stop/target populated the R-path (like the order block).
- **Result — the wedge has real, measured edge:** POSITIVE forward-return edge vs random-entry-in-tape at **every** horizon — **+0.14 / +0.24 / +0.74 / +1.03%** at 5/10/20/60d, hit-rate ~58–62% (rising with horizon), n=256. **R-expectancy +0.14R** (46.5% target-hit before stop; target=119 / stop=134 / open=3). avg MAE −8.1%.
- **Significance:** of all scanners measured so far, **only Falling Wedge and Harmonic Reversal beat baseline** — and the wedge is the **strongest at 20d/60d** (Harmonic was +0.16..+0.67% @5-20d; wedge +0.14..+1.03% and still climbing at 60d). Order Block / RSI-60-40 / VCP / Structural Breakout all showed ≤0 forward-edge. Updated the harness findings table + key-insight, [[analyses/Falling-Wedge-Scanner-Fit]] (all 4 phases complete), [[concepts/Falling-Wedge]].
- **Honesty caveats (unchanged):** gross (no costs/slippage), survivorship-biased (today's universe), one ~6y mostly-constructive window → **directional, not proof**. ## [2026-07-25] build | FW-1 SHIPPED — Falling Wedge scanner
- Built `scan_falling_wedge(df)` in [scanner_templates.py](tools/scanner_templates.py) from the ingested algorithm ([[concepts/Falling-Wedge]] / [[analyses/Falling-Wedge-Scanner-Fit]]), registered **"Falling Wedge"** (Pattern, 24 scanners now) → live in **both** the Scanner Hub and Opportunity Hub as cards.
- **Logic:** last 60 bars → pivot swings (look-back 1) → `np.polyfit` upper line (last ≤3 swing highs) + lower line (last ≤3 swing lows) → geometry gates (both slopes negative, **upper steeper**, span ≥15 bars, gap **converging** ≥10%, **containment ≥55%**, ≥2 touches/side) → confidence 0-100 (containment 0.30 · convergence 0.25 · touches 0.20 · steepness 0.15 · recency 0.10 + state bonus) → **breakout state** (Breakout close>upper / Pre-Breakout within 0.5% / Forming in the upper half; deep-in-wedge → no fire). Structured **entry/stop/target** (enter on/above the upper line, stop below the lower line, target = measured move = widest gap) → also backtest-ready via the harness R-path. Emits `score_base`+`rs_raw` for the RS blend.
- **Verified:** compile clean; **ITC live = clean confirmed Breakout** (conf 100, containment 97%, 6/4 touches, 63% convergence, 1.7× vol → `run_scanner` graded **87.5 / Very Strong**). **Caught & fixed a false positive** (DABUR passed geometry at only 28% containment) by adding the **containment ≥55% gate** → DABUR correctly dropped. Synthetic non-crossing wedge fires all three states (Forming/Pre-Breakout/Breakout, containment 98-100%); negatives reject (uptrend, <60 bars, crossed-apex gap≤0). Dashboard boots clean (HTTP 200). **Restart start.bat** to use it.
- Not matching the source screenshot's Jul-21 hits (TATACONSUM/WIPRO/NMDC/…) is expected — that scan was days old and those wedges have since resolved. **Next:** FW-2 (volume dry-up gate + richer state metrics), FW-3 (chart overlay on `patternCanvas`), FW-4 (backtest the edge).

## [2026-07-25] build | Dashboard Phase 3 — Backtest Harness (engine) SHIPPED
- Built [backtest.py](tools/backtest.py) — the measurement layer for [[analyses/Dashboard-UI-Roadmap]] Phase 3, the roadmap's flagged #1 credibility upgrade. Full methodology + findings: [[analyses/Backtest-Harness]].
- **Point-in-time replay, look-ahead-safe by construction:** at bar `t` the scanner sees only `df.iloc[:t+1]`; entry at the **next bar's open**; forward returns at 5/10/20/60 bars. **Baseline = every-bar forward return** over the same names/window, so *edge* = signal return − random-entry-in-tape. De-dups clustered signals by `min_gap`. Generic — works on all 22 scanners with zero per-scanner code.
- **R-expectancy** via `_simulate_plan` (stop-or-target-first, pessimistic same-bar tie → stop). **Unit-verified** (target=+2R, stop=−1R, tie→stop). Dormant in practice: scanners bury their plans in reason-text, not structured `entry`/`stop`/`target` fields → the clear follow-on is to expose those fields.
- **Verified live on 5 scanners** (49-name liquid universe, 1500 bars, ~90s/scanner headless): **Harmonic Reversal = the only positive edge** (+0.16..+0.67% @5–20d, 60% hit, n=245); RSI 60-40 (−0.5%, n=1766), VCP Coiling (−0.44%, n=1994), Structural Breakout (−0.45%, n=1137) all ≤ baseline. So most hand-set scanners **don't beat a coin-flip entry** in this tape — exactly the check the roadmap wanted. Caveats: gross, survivorship-biased, one ~6y window → directional, not a verdict.
- Run headless: `python tools/backtest.py "RSI 60-40 Setup"`. py_compile clean. **Still open:** structured-plan fields (unlocks R), a dashboard Ops-tab surface (needs run-caching + progress), and multi-window/walk-forward + costs before retuning any threshold.

## [2026-07-25] build | Dashboard Phase 2 — RSI 60-40 Setup scanner SHIPPED
- Built the RSI 60-40 with-trend continuation scanner from [[strategies/RSI-60-40-Setup]] — the first Phase 2 item of [[analyses/Dashboard-UI-Roadmap]]. New `scan_rsi_60_40(df)` + `_rsi_series()` (Wilder RSI) in [scanner_templates.py](tools/scanner_templates.py), registered as **"RSI 60-40 Setup"** (Entry), so it surfaces in **both** the Scanner Hub and Opportunity Hub (22 scanners now).
- **Logic:** a trend-regime gate (price above EMA21 & EMA50, EMA21 stacked over EMA50, and RSI(14) oscillating in a real ~40-60 band — regime rejected if recent RSI min<30, max>80, or median<40) then one of two entries: **A. RSI-40 Pullback** (RSI 36–50 turning up at EMA support, stop < EMA50) or **B. RSI-60 Breakout** (RSI breaking >60 clearing the 20-day high on volume, stop < EMA21). Each hit carries a 2R trade plan + volume-confirmation ⚠ when < 1.5×. Emits `score_base`+`rs_raw` so the hub RS-blends the grade like other scanners.
- **Verified live + synthetic:** M&M fired **RSI-60 Breakout** (base 69.3, correctly ⚠-flagged 1.17× thin volume, plan entry 3230/stop 3121/target 3447); `run_scanner` blended it to **score 64.5 / Moderate** (ring renders). Branch A confirmed via a param sweep (base 76, confirmation bar + plan). Negatives reject: downtrend → None, <70 bars → None, RSI-pinned-100 (regime gate) → None. 1 live hit / 29 is genuine — Nifty below 200-SMA, so few names hold a clean 40-60 uptrend (gate is discriminating). **Restart start.bat** to use it.

## [2026-07-24] fix | screener_login crashed on Windows console (UnicodeEncodeError) — the real Phase-3 blocker
- **Root cause of "stuck":** `screener_login.py` printed box-drawing (`─`), check-mark (`✓`/`✗`) and ellipsis (`…`) glyphs to stdout. A default Windows console is cp1252 and **can't encode them → `UnicodeEncodeError` aborted the launcher at the first status print**, right after opening the browser and *before* login could happen. The three prior 2026-07-24 entries only "verified compile," so the tool was never actually run in a real terminal and this crash was never caught — which is why no `screener_session.json` was ever produced.
- **Fix:** added `_force_utf8_console()` (best-effort `sys.stdout/stderr.reconfigure(encoding="utf-8", errors="replace")`), called at the top of `main()`. One-shot fix for every Unicode print in the file; safe on terminals lacking the glyph (replacement char, no crash).
- **Verified:** re-ran `--headless --timeout 18` → no crash, clean output, correct "timed out, no logged-in session" (the persistent `pw_screener_profile` has a Cookies DB but **no completed screener.in login**). Default-browser detection returns `edge`. Capture itself still needs the user (log in once, or Option B with Edge closed) — credentials never handled here.

## [2026-07-24] roadmap | PEAD Phase 5 — Quality-gated Small-Cap Proxy-PEAD
- User request: a scanner for **small-cap stocks that are Zero Debt + High ROE/ROCE/OPM**, carrying the **PEAD drift advantage**. Filed as **Phase 5** in [[analyses/PEAD-Scanner-Roadmap]] (not a new page — an addition to the existing scanner roadmap, per the request "include it to existing to-do list").
- **Key finding — both halves already exist:** the quality screen is literally already coded in [multibagger_candidates.py](tools/multibagger_candidates.py) (screener.in query: `Mkt cap < 20000, Debt/equity < 1, ROCE > 15, OPM > 10`) and the High-Growth Screener page has ROE/ROCE/OPM slider gates; the drift engine is `scan_pead`. So this is a **composition**, not new research.
- **Architectural decision — combine at the universe level, not inside the scanner.** `run_scanner` is symbol-blind (`spec["fn"](df)` runs on OHLCV; symbol attached after), so fundamentals can't be read per bar. But the quality gate is a symbol property → apply it as a **pre-filter on the symbol list**, then run Proxy-PEAD over survivors. Sidesteps the exact plumbing wall that made Phase-3 SUE hard.
- **Sub-phases:** 5.1 quality-universe → Proxy-PEAD (MVP, *no scanner-code change*); 5.2 dedicated "Quality Small-Cap PEAD" scanner + fundamentals/quality-tier on the card (rides Phase-3 join); 5.3 backtest whether the quality gate lifts PEAD expectancy (rides Phase 4).
- **Honesty guardrails carried forward:** still Proxy-PEAD (price-inferred event, not true SUE); "Zero Debt" operationalised as Debt/equity < 0.1; fundamentals are latest-quarter screener.in (can be a quarter stale); keep volume/SUV gates so illiquid small-caps don't score. Nothing shipped yet — this is a roadmap/to-do entry only.

## [2026-07-24] build | screener_login Option B (--use-profile) — reuse real browser SSO, no admin
- Added `--use-profile`: Playwright `launch_persistent_context` against the user's REAL browser User-Data dir, so it loads the existing (SSO) screener.in session and reads the cookie the browser itself decrypted — sidesteps Windows app-bound encryption entirely (no admin) and no re-login. Only requirement: the browser is CLOSED (profile lock); the launch error is caught with a "close it fully" hint.
- Auto-detects the Windows default browser from the registry (`_detect_default_browser` → **this machine = Edge**); override via `--browser chrome|edge|brave` / `--profile`. Brave via `executable_path`, Chrome/Edge via Playwright channels. Firefox default → routed to `--from-browser` (Firefox has no app-bound encryption). Captured cookie is verified logged-in before caching.
- `screener_login.bat` now drives Option B (no self-elevation), with a close-the-browser prompt. Verified: compile; default-browser detection returns 'edge'; all three profile dirs resolve; real-profile launch not auto-run (user's action — avoids opening their live browser unprompted).

## [2026-07-24] fix | screener_login: add default-browser reuse (--from-browser) — no re-login
- The initial launcher opened its OWN fresh Playwright profile, so screener/Google saw a logged-out browser and demanded a full SSO login (passkey) — it did NOT reuse the user's default-browser session. Design flaw.
- Added `--from-browser` mode + `read_from_local_browsers()` in `browser_cookie_helper`: reads the sessionid the DEFAULT browser already holds (existing SSO session, no re-login, no window). On Windows this needs an ELEVATED terminal (Chrome/Edge/Brave app-bound cookie encryption → `RequiresAdminError`); the mode surfaces the per-browser reason + admin hint instead of failing silently. `screener_login.bat` now self-elevates (UAC) and runs this path; the Playwright login-window remains the fallback if newest Chrome ABE defeats even the admin read.
- Verified: compile; non-admin `--from-browser` correctly lists Chrome/Edge/Brave `RequiresAdminError` + admin guidance and writes no cache.

## [2026-07-24] build | screener.in session auto-capture launcher (unblocks Phase-3 SUE)
- **Root cause found:** the existing `browser_cookie_helper.py` (auto-reads the screener.in `sessionid` from local browsers, already wired into 3 dashboard screener pages) silently returns nothing on this machine — Chrome/Edge/Brave raise `RequiresAdminError` (Windows app-bound cookie encryption, Chrome 127+). So the dashboard's screener features were quietly session-less.
- **New launcher** `tools/screener_login.py` + root `screener_login.bat`: opens screener.in in a **persistent Playwright profile**, the user logs in themselves (password or Google SSO — never automated; no credential handling), then it reads the `sessionid` from the browser context, **verifies it's a logged-in session** (reuses the scraper's `/login/`-redirect auth check), and caches it to `tools/cache/screener_session.json` (20-day trust cap). Persistent profile → after first login, re-running refreshes in seconds.
- **Patched** `get_screener_sessionid()` to read that cache FIRST, then fall back to the (encryption-blocked) browser read. Returns source `"saved login"`.
- **Verified:** compile; bogus cookie → not-authenticated (live network check); cache save→read→stale-guard (40d ignored)→cleanup roundtrip; Playwright headless smoke test loaded the real screener.in login page ("Login - Screener"), read cookies, correctly declined to cache an anonymous pre-login session. Interactive login itself is user-run (credentials stay with the user).
- **Why it matters:** removes the session-gating friction that made Phase-3 real-SUE look impractical. With a valid cached session, `fetch_company_page` can pull screener.in's ~12–13 quarters of EPS (enough to z-score a true time-series SUE) in the daily batch. See [[analyses/PEAD-Scanner-Roadmap]]. Dashboard restart needed to load the patched helper.

## [2026-07-24] build | Proxy-PEAD Phase 1.1 — SUV z-score + pre-D0 baseline
- Absorbed the two low-effort wins from [[sources/TV-PEAD-Screener-PineScript]] into `scan_pead` ([scanner_templates.py](tools/scanner_templates.py)).
- **SUV (Standardized Unexpected Volume):** new `_suv_series()` helper — a per-bar volume z-score vs a `shift(1)`-rolling **pre-event baseline** (anti-leak: the event bar never inflates the mean/std it's judged against), raw or log mode, 0.2σ floor, `[-10,20]` clamp. The event gate is now **SUV ≥ 1.5σ** instead of a raw ×-median ratio; SUV shown as a `SUV n.nσ` tag + reason. `vol_x50d` still emitted for the "Vol Confirmed" pill.
- **Verified:** unit test on a synthetic volume spike (SUV clamps to 20σ; anti-leak confirmed — baseline excludes the spike bar) + live NSE re-run through `run_scanner`: HCLTECH (SUV 3.2σ, +11%/15d, score 79), TCS (2.2σ, +3%/9d, 64). **INFY correctly dropped** — 1.66× median but <1.5σ vs its own baseline, i.e. the z-score is more discriminating than the ratio (the point). 0 NaN-corrupted.
- **Honest correction on scope:** I'd billed **time-series SUE** as another low-effort win — it is NOT. `scan_pead` is *symbol-blind* (`run_scanner` attaches the symbol only AFTER the fn runs, so it can't fetch EPS), and yfinance `quarterly_income_stmt` gives only ~6 quarters (~1–2 YoY diffs — too thin to z-score). Forcing it = fake precision. SUE stays Phase 3, needs deeper EPS (screener.in) + a plumbing change. Recorded in [[analyses/PEAD-Scanner-Roadmap]] (Absorption table + 2026-07-24 update).

## [2026-07-23] build | M5 Phase 1 — Proxy-PEAD Drift scanner (Scanner Hub)
- New scanner `scan_pead()` in [scanner_templates.py](tools/scanner_templates.py), registered in `SCANNERS` (now 17), category Momentum. Renders as **cards** in the Scanner Hub 3-column grid (per the standing card-presentation rule, not a table).
- **What it does (price-only, NSE-honest):** NSE has no free analyst-consensus estimates (documented in [screener_scraper.py](tools/screener_scraper.py) — screener.in returns nothing for "surprise"), so a true SUE is not computable. Instead the earnings event is *inferred from price*: a large high-volume up-reaction (≥1.5×ATR14, ≥4% close-to-close, ≥1.5× 50-day median volume within the last 20 sessions), and the scanner fires only while the move is still **holding/extending** — the drift. Emits `vol_x50d` so the Hub "Vol Confirmed" pill works.
- **Honesty guardrail (deliberate):** labelled **"Proxy-PEAD"** everywhere (pattern type, description, and a literal per-card reason line: "event inferred from the price gap, not a confirmed earnings date"). Never "SUE" or "earnings surprise %". Mirrors the M3 anti-overtrust discipline.
- **Verified live through the real `run_scanner` path** (not just unit tests) over 30 liquid NSE names during Q1 earnings season → 3 clean, correct hits: **HCLTECH** (+5.6% gap → +10% drift over 15 sessions, score 78), **TCS** (+5.4% gap, +3% over 9d, 65), **INFY** (+5.6% gap, +1% over 16d, 56) — all real Q1 IT-major post-earnings drifts. Plus 4 synthetic gate tests (fire / drift-faded / no-event / low-volume) all correct.
- **Bug caught by live data + fixed:** ULTRACEMCO fired with "nan% gap", score 100 — a NaN yfinance price bar bypassed every gate (NaN comparisons are always False). Fixed with an up-front `dropna(subset=OHLCV).reset_index()` plus in-loop `np.isnan(react)` and a `last` NaN guard. Re-run → 0 NaN-corrupted hits.
- **Not yet done (planned phases):** Phase 2 = reconcile the inferred event against real yfinance earnings dates (needs `lxml`); Phase 3 = overlay YoY quarterly profit growth + add an ADX filter (the one genuine gap vs the source text — ADX is not yet in the templates); Phase 4 = backtest the drift edge on NSE to replace hand-set thresholds. Full record: [[analyses/PEAD-Scanner-Roadmap]].
- Note: the running dashboard (start.bat, port 8501) predates this edit — restart start.bat to see "Proxy-PEAD Drift" in the Scanner Hub dropdown.

## [2026-07-23] build | M3 verified + projection engine anti-overtrust fixes
- **M3 (price-projection line) verified end-to-end and hardened.** The build itself (projection.py + /api/projection + chart overlay) had landed in `e123915`; this session's live verification **caught a real overtrust bug**: the chart client fetches 2600 daily bars (10Y), EW ran over the full decade → TITAN printed **"+137.9% High confidence"**.
- Three fixes in [projection.py](tools/projection.py): (1) `ANALYSIS_BARS=300` — fixed analysis window, so 2600-bar and 300-bar requests now produce identical projections; (2) `MAX_MOVE_PCT` per-timeframe plausibility cap (daily >60% rejected as a candidate); (3) EW effective confidence = count_conf × (0.4 + 0.6×target_prob) — a 35%-prob Fib target can no longer surface as "High" on wave-count confidence alone.
- Post-fix verification: TITAN → **Medium 58, VCP ₹5,409.76 (+15.2%), R:R 3.39×, stop ₹4,487.05** rendered in-browser (slope + label + panel + disclaimer); SUNPHARMA tempered High 90 → Medium 60; RELIANCE null case → 0 painted px, panel hidden; no console errors.

- **Key insight (resolves a standing contradiction):** DGT exposes **fill logic as a user setting with 4 definitions** — Any Touch / Midpoint / Wick Sweep / Body Beyond. "Any Touch" = LuxAlgo's wick-through retire, "Body Beyond" = the Quant script's close-through invalidation. The wiki's long-running wick-vs-close mitigation contradiction is just the two ends of one `fill_mode` dial → port it to `tools/fvg.js` as a parameter, not a hard-coded rule.
- **Second new capability:** three-source detection = current TF + HTF + **LTF/intrabar** (Auto-selected lower timeframe). LuxAlgo only projects HTF *down*; DGT also reads gaps hidden *inside* the current bar.

- Source: YouTube transcript by [[Kaushik-Akkiwakar]] (SEBI RA) covering TSI from first principles — formula (double-smoothed ΔClose over 25 then 13, ×100), 13/7-EMA signal line, 4-quadrant trend×momentum matrix using [[SuperTrend]] (or MAST = MA+SuperTrend) as trend filter, TSI vs [[RSI]] comparison, six worked examples on [[Point-and-Figure-Charts]], zero-line confirmation, and the author's live deployment at [[MarkUp-Platform]] on daily + 5-min timeframes.
- Filed:
  - [[sources/YT-Kaushik-Akkiwakar-TSI-Momentum]] — source summary with 4-quadrant matrix and 6 examples.
  - [[concepts/True-Strength-Index-TSI]] — first-class concept page with formula derivation, RSI contrast table, signal taxonomy, best pairings, common mistakes.

  - [[entities/Kaushik-Akkiwakar]] — SEBI RA entity page.
  - [[entities/MarkUp-Platform]] — signals platform entity page.
- **Key insight**: TSI is a *trend-momentum confluence* tool, not a standalone oscillator. Reading TSI crossovers conditional on trend (via SuperTrend) is the entire edge — a bullish trend + negative TSI crossover is a pause/profit-book signal, not a reversal.
- **Local fit / actionable**: TSI slots naturally as a 5th chart pane alongside [[MACD-Divergence]] and [[RSI]] (same "oscillator below price" convention established after `87a49c6` RS Line and `fcb3faa` MACD). Scanner rule "negative TSI crossover in bullish trend" fits the SwingEdge Momentum Buildup family. Check redundancy against [[KST]] before adding both.
- **Contradictions**: none with existing wiki. Author's "95% of traders don't know TSI" is rhetorical; no backtest stats given for the 4-quadrant matrix.

## [2026-07-08] create | strategies/Humbled-Trader-Trend-Join-Long (US cash pre-market gapper)
- Extracted the day-trade rule set from [[sources/PreMarketTool-repo]] (`WATCHLIST_CRITERIA.md`) into a first-class strategy page. Filled the wiki's Strategy convention in full: Core Idea / Setup Criteria / Entry Rules / Exit Rules / Timeframe / Best & Worst Conditions / Backtest / Examples / Related / Contradictions.
- **Setup Criteria table** (all six required): gap > 3% · price > $3 · mcap > $1B · pre-market RVOL > 1.5 · price above prior HOD · catalyst identified (else auto-SKIP per the repo's Claude+Codex prompt guardrails).
- **Entry:** trade window opens 10:00 ET (30-min buffer avoids opening chop); trigger = break of PMH **and** prior HOD together (either alone insufficient). Both stop-order and limit-at-PMH entry mechanics supported.
- **Exit ladder:** stop = 1% below PMH or LOD, whichever lower (= 1R); ⅓ off at +1R, ⅓ at +2R, trail last ⅓ on 21-EMA; **hard time-flat at 15:51 ET** — mechanical, not discretionary.
- **Backtest preserved:** 54.6% WR / PF 1.59 / 280 trades (flagged as un-audited — sample period, universe, and slippage not documented in the repo; noted as gap).
- **Cross-links:** [[concepts/VWAP]] as compatible confluence check on entry day, [[concepts/EMA]] for the 21-EMA trail logic, [[strategies/Koroush-Breakout-Momentum]] as the crypto-side analogue (same "reject fast-spike approaches" philosophy).
- **Contradictions flagged**: rules are US large-cap cash equity only — do NOT port the 1% PMH stop, PMH+HOD trigger, or 15:51 ET time-flat to Indian equity swings or crypto without independent re-testing. The "flat by 15:51 ET, no overnight" rule is a *scope* difference (day-trade vs swing), not a real conflict.
- Index updated (156→157 pages, sources unchanged at 66; Strategies table row added under Koroush-Breakout-Momentum).

## [2026-07-03] ingest | Pine Script — Multi Timeframe Volume Profiles [TradingIQ] (KioseffTrading)
- Trigger: Kalyan pasted the Pine v6 source — fourth item in today's ingest-and-adopt series for the Early Reversal validation layer.
- **What's genuinely new**: up to 5 simultaneous HTF volume profiles (30m→1W) built from *lower-timeframe* volume (`request.security_lower_tf`), the on-chart embodiment of MTF value confluence; a Delta model signing volume by tick rule with a graceful upgrade to real bid/ask matching on tick charts; POC + 70% VA per profile with a live "where is price vs value per timeframe" dot.
- **Two methodological weaknesses flagged (contradictions with our own module)**: even-split volume allocation per row (vs `volume_profile.js`'s proportional range overlap) and alternating VA expansion (vs the standard volume-greedy rule) — both can distort the profile/VAH/VAL. The wiki's existing methods are better; the Pine's value is MTF stacking + delta + LTF sourcing.
- **Adopted with the better math**: `compute_volume_profile_levels()` in `tools/scanner_templates.py` — POC/VAH/VAL + per-bin delta, proportional allocation, greedy VA expansion (deviations documented in the docstring). Wired as the 9th validation check ("Profile"): close accepted above POC passes (noting above-entire-VA and buy-side delta at the node when true); close below VAL raises ⚠ "no volume acceptance of the recovery yet". Verified: recovery scenario passes above POC (78.85, above whole VA, positive delta), heavy-base-then-slide scenario correctly warns below VAL.

## [2026-07-03] ingest | Pine Script — BOS & FVG Pullback Signals (Quant)
- Trigger: Kalyan pasted the Pine v6 source for ingest — third item in today's ingest-and-adopt series for the Early Reversal validation layer.
- **Contradiction flagged first**: the title claims BOS but the code contains zero market-structure logic — it's a pure FVG detector + pullback-rejection entry signal. Filed under FVG accordingly.
- **What's worth keeping**: quality-filtered FVG definition (directional middle candle + gap > 0.5×ATR(14) — auto-adapts to volatility, unlike `fvg.js`'s optional fixed-% filter); newest-unmitigated-zone-per-side tracking; full-close-through-only invalidation (partial fill = the entry, not the kill); 3-condition pullback rejection — touch inside zone + close back above with dominant lower wick + *opened* above the zone (anti-gap-down trap, same trapped-trader spirit as yesterday's body-close trendline rule).
- **Quirk found while testing the port**: the zone top is candle 3's own low, so touch is trivially true on the formation bar — a hammer-shaped candle 3 fires BUY and consumes its zone with no pullback ever happening. Documented on the source page; port reproduces it faithfully.
- **Adopted**: `detect_fvg_pullback()` in `tools/scanner_templates.py`, wired as the 8th validation check ("FVG") — recent bullish FVG rejection passes, recent bearish rejection raises a ⚠ warning. Verified on hand-built bars (signal fired exactly on the pullback bar) alongside the full regression of the other 7 checks.
- Pages created: sources/BOS-FVG-Pullback-Signals-Pine.md, concepts/Fair-Value-Gap.md (first dedicated FVG page; wiki now 135 pages, 54 sources)

## [2026-07-03] ingest | Pine Script — Trend Lines by Kris (KRISTRADER1)
- Trigger: Kalyan pasted the Pine v6 source for ingest, following the validation-layer build earlier today (the "will provide Pine logic to ingest and adopt" path from that request).
- **Core mechanics worth keeping**: fully mechanical trendlines from confirmed pivots (supply = falling pivot highs, demand = rising pivot lows, ≤7×prd bars apart); clean-line validation (rejected if any close already crossed it); normalized angle filter ≤30° (slope mapped onto a 500-bar 3:1-aspect box → scale-independent steepness cap); break requires the candle *body* to close beyond the line — wick pierces don't count, which independently converges on the False-Break-Buildup-Trading wick-vs-body false-break rule.
- **Adopted, not just filed**: ported as `detect_trendline_break()` in `tools/scanner_templates.py` and wired as the 7th validation check ("Trendline") — recent supply-line break passes (bullish: downtrend's supply structure objectively ended, the diagonal cousin of a CHOCH), recent demand-line break raises a ⚠ warning on Early Reversal cards. High-Growth Screener Validations column picks it up automatically (TL✓/✗).
- Verified on a synthetic staircase-decline → breakout: correctly *rejected* a steep staircase via the angle filter (working as designed), detected the break on gentle geometry (20.5° line, break 6 bars ago → 6/7 validations).
- Pages created: sources/Trend-Lines-by-Kris-Pine.md, concepts/Trend-Lines.md (first dedicated trendline concept page; wiki now 133 pages, 53 sources)
- Pages updated: concepts/Market-Structure-CHOCH-BOS.md (cross-link: diagonal supply-line break vs horizontal swing break), index.md

## [2026-07-03] build | Six-check validation layer for the Early Reversal Detector + High-Growth Screener
- Trigger: Kalyan asked for six additional validation measures on every Early Reversal scanner card and in the screener logic (alongside the existing %R Trend Exhaustion), sourced from the vault where Python-equivalent logic exists.
- Vault coverage check: 4 of 6 validators ported directly from existing wiki pages — concepts/Market-Structure-CHOCH-BOS.md (structure), concepts/Multi-Timeframe-Analysis.md (weekly-regime alignment), strategies/False-Break-Buildup-Trading.md (fakeout/retest), strategies/TPT-Thrust-Pullback-Thrust.md (pullback-after-breakout). Volume confirmation (breakout-volume rule + up/down volume ratio, per Stage-Analysis/Minervini) and the ATR volatility band are textbook — no Pine ingest needed.
- Implemented in `tools/scanner_templates.py`: `detect_bos_choch` (SMC swing-labeling walk; bearish break invalidates a prior bullish event), `detect_volume_confirmation` (≥1.5× RVOL up-day or 10-day up/down volume ratio ≥1.2), `compute_atr_filter` (ATR% in 1–8% band + contraction flag), `detect_mtf_alignment` (weekly resample: 10-wk EMA / weekly RSI ≥45 / weekly higher low, pass = 2 of 3), `detect_pullback_entry` (breakout → pullback held → turning up), `detect_fakeout` (false break vs confirmed retest), plus `compute_validation_summary` combining all six.

High-Growth Screener gained a compact "Validations" column (`5/6 · Str✓ Vol✓ ATR✓ MTF✓ PB✓ RT✗`) next to the Exhaustion column.
- Verified with a synthetic decline→base→breakout→pullback scenario (5/6 checks fired correctly) plus edge cases (short history, missing volume, flat prices, non-datetime index all degrade gracefully).

## [2026-07-02] build | Early Reversal Detector (Scanner Hub)
- Implements the detector proposed across the 4 reversal-signal ingests earlier today. Lives in `tools/scanner_templates.py`, registered in `SCANNERS` as "Early Reversal Detector" (category Entry) — reuses all of Scanner Hub's existing universe-selection UI and the warm-themed result cards.
- **Composite of all 4 signals**: `compute_drop_recovery_signal()` (gate — must show a real decline + partial reclaim, fixes the Upside-Reversal-Screener's likely highBeforeLow bug by using a genuine N-day rolling high) → `compute_recent_exhaustion_signal()` (reused as-is from the Pre-Breakout/High-Growth screeners) → `detect_bullish_candlestick()` (new: Bullish Engulfing/Hammer/Bullish Harami/Piercing Line/Morning Star, geometry-scored per the Forthius ingest) + `detect_bullish_rsi_divergence()` (new, cheap) → `detect_bullish_reversal_structure()` (new: simplified Double Bottom/Inverse H&S, pivot-based per the AGPro ingest). Combined into `compute_early_reversal_signal()`, 0-100 score, ≥35 to surface as a card.
- **Applied the Forthius design lesson directly**: no leg of this requires a strict prior downtrend — the drop-recovery gate alone establishes "a real decline happened," so the detector doesn't go quiet during the ranging Stage 4→1→2 transition window the way Forthius's own default config does.
- **Tested against live data before trusting it**: ran all 4 sub-functions + the composite against 9 real NSE symbols via yfinance directly (bypassing Streamlit) — zero exceptions, plausible drop/recovery percentages, Double Bottom quality scores 6.7-9.0 on 3 genuine candidates (Goodyear India, HDFCBank, SBIN). Caught and fixed a calibration issue this way: the initial ≥40 threshold made it nearly impossible for a single non-exhaustion signal to qualify alone (SBIN's genuinely strong 9.0/10 Double Bottom scored 38.5, just under the bar) — lowered to ≥35. Confirmed the fix in the actual dashboard UI afterward (Scanner Hub → Early Reversal Detector → SBIN/HDFCBANK both surfaced correctly).
- Not built: TradingView-native screener-column distribution (would need checking Kalyan's plan tier) and the full Forthius/AGPro pattern catalogs (scoped down to the 5 most common candlestick patterns and 2 of the 4 chart-pattern types, per the "reuse the idea, not the whole script" principle already applied throughout this thread).

## [2026-07-02] ingest | Pine Script — Upside Reversal Screener
- Trigger: Kalyan pasted the full Pine v6 source directly — 4th reversal-detection script this session, and by far the simplest: no candlestick shapes, no chart patterns, no oscillator, just drop % (or ATR-multiple) from a recent high then recovery % of that drop reclaimed. New [[strategies/Drop-Recovery-Reversal-Screen]] strategy page, cross-linked as the mirror image of the existing [[strategies/Parabolic-Short-First-Red-Day]].
- **New distribution mechanism, not just new logic**: unlike the other 3 scripts (visual chart overlays), this one's `isScreenerMode` output is raw numeric columns (Open/High/Low/Close/Change%/Drop%/Recovery%/Reversed) meant for TradingView's own native Stock Screener, filterable whole-market with no external plumbing (Chartink/screener.in/Python) at all — though custom-script screener columns are typically a paid TradingView tier, not confirmed available on Kalyan's plan. Flagged as worth checking directly before assuming a Python port is needed.
- **Flagged a likely implementation issue, not silently trusted**: the `highBeforeLow` lookback loop only accumulates while consecutive bars share the same calendar day (daily)/week (weekly) — a condition that appears to break immediately at the first iteration on *both* timeframes the script is restricted to (`runtime.error` blocks intraday). If correct, `highBeforeLow` collapses to just `max(prior close, today's open)` rather than a meaningful recent swing high — reads like leftover logic from an intraday-session-aggregation version. Documented as a static-reading concern requiring live verification, not a confirmed bug.
- Proposed role: the cheapest possible pre-filter for the Early Reversal Detector — pure OHLCV magnitude check ahead of the more expensive candlestick/chart-pattern/oscillator signals, not a replacement for them.
- Pages created: sources/Upside-Reversal-Screener-Pine.md, strategies/Drop-Recovery-Reversal-Screen.md (wiki now 127 pages, 48 sources)

## [2026-07-02] ingest | Live chart — Forthius Reversal Scanner v5 running on 6yr Goodyear India daily
- Trigger: Kalyan shared a second screenshot, this time of the actual rendered scanner over 2020-2026, asking to note anything new about the *visual/runtime* implementation beyond the source-code read already ingested.
- **Most important finding — a real gap for the Early Reversal Detector design**: at the current bar (the exact low/recovery point the earlier %R Trend Exhaustion screenshot flagged bullish), the dashboard reads **Trend: RANGE**, not DOWN — and since bullish candlestick patterns require `downCtx = downTrend or (relaxTrend and ranging)` with `relaxTrend` defaulting false, Forthius's own default config would suppress bullish signals at exactly the Stage 4→1→2 transition moment this whole thread is trying to catch. Documented as a design rule in [[concepts/Stage-Analysis]]: any detector built here must treat "ranging after a decline" as first-class permissive, not opt-in.
- **Near S/R also lags at the worst moment**: shows "—" at the current bar despite sitting at an obvious multi-year low, because pivot confirmation needs `pvtLen` (5) bars of look-ahead — a pivot at the exact low can't confirm until 5+ bars later.
- **No label de-overlap exists** in this script (unlike AGPro's) — confirmed visually: 3 labels stack/overlap in the dense 2026-low reversal zone.
- **Grade distribution in real use skews low**: only 1 of ~16 visible signals across 6 years reached grade A; most are B/C. Bearish Engulfing fires far more often than any other pattern (loosest geometric bar to clear).
- **Convergence evidence**: bullish candlestick cluster (BullHarami ×2, 3Soldiers) lines up on the same bar as the earlier-ingested bullish %R Trend Exhaustion signal and an 8.6× volume spike — three independently-built signals agreeing strengthens the case for combining them rather than picking one.
- Pages updated: sources/Forthius-Reversal-Scanner-Pine.md (new Live Behavior Observations section), concepts/Stage-Analysis.md (design-rule note)
